# Behavior checklist

This is Clawtrate's definition of done. Each row is one behavior that a single automated check can verify.

Columns:

- **id**: stable. Never renumber. New rows get the next free id at the end.
- **area**: the part of Clawtrate the row belongs to.
- **platform**: where Clawtrate must pass the check: `desktop`, `iOS` or `both`.
- **behavior**: what happens, in one or two sentences.
- **target**: what the check verifies. `same` means the behavior cell as written. A note after `same` narrows the scope; it does not change the behavior. `better:` is followed by the target in full, where it adds to or changes the behavior cell; where the two differ, the target governs. Use Clawtrate's own names, art and branding.
  `not planned:` marks a behavior that needs a hosted service Clawtrate does not run. Builders skip `not planned` rows. The dependency pass has turned those the gateway cannot replace into `gap`, with the evidence in the gateway dependency cell; a `not planned` row that is still `todo` has a self-hosted equivalent, named in its cell.
- **gateway dependency**: what the gateway gives the row, from `docs/research/gateway-capabilities.md`. Each cell starts with a class: `client only` (nothing from the gateway beyond data another row already loads), `available` (methods and events the map names and describes cover every part of it), `partial` (they cover part, and the cell says what is missing), `plugin` (it needs a plugin from the capability map; the cell says whether the map demonstrated or only proposes it, and what works without it) or `none` (it cannot be done, and the cell says why). Then come the methods and events it uses, the operator scope, and any condition under which a call needs `operator.admin`. The scope is the tier Clawtrate requests for that behavior: `operator.read`, then `operator.write`, then `operator.admin`, plus `operator.approvals`, `operator.questions` or `operator.pairing` where a method needs them. Clawtrate does not use the narrower `operator.sessions.read` and `operator.sessions.write`, even where a method admits them, because they depend on sharing and on which sessions the caller owns, and a personal client must see every Bot and task on the gateway. "not in the capability map" marks a need the map does not yet cover, and **unverified** is carried over from the map. The capability map is the authority for this column: a builder who finds a dependency cell wrong corrects it in the same change.
- **status**: `todo`, then `done` with a passing check, or `gap` with evidence that it cannot be done without breaking a rule. A `both` row whose desktop check passes while its iOS check is still to come is `desktop done`; the value is used on `both` rows only. A check names the rows it covers at the start of its title (`F-013 F-015: …`), and `pnpm test` fails when a `done` or `desktop done` row has no such check.

Fine-grained control requirements are tagged in the target column. Each names a kind of control a person has over their bots:

- `FC-01`: the model and reasoning effort of each bot, and a record of what each turn used.
- `FC-02`: where a bot runs and what it can reach: its machine, browser profile, folders, connectors and saved logins.
- `FC-03`: memory a person can see, edit, export, import and clear.
- `FC-04`: instructions a person can read and edit.
- `FC-05`: every action shown, with the raw call behind it.
- `FC-06`: many tasks for each bot, each with its own status, context and history.
- `FC-07`: usage by turn, task, bot and routine, and budgets.
- `FC-08`: a Stop that works, and a clear account of the connection and the machine.
- `FC-09`: approvals and rules that can apply to one bot, and standing approvals that are listed and can be revoked.
- `FC-10`: control over bots messaging each other.
- `FC-11`: check-ins a person can turn off or schedule.
- `FC-12`: notification settings for each bot and task, and quiet hours.
- `FC-13`: routines that can be read and edited directly.
- `FC-14`: deleting a bot says what goes and what stays.
- `FC-15`: failures are never silent.

Where `docs/design/` specifies how an interaction looks and moves, that specification governs appearance, and this table governs what must be true.

Approval rows describe their fixture in one line with five fields, always in this order: `gateway denial`, `deny`, `ask`, `allow` and `review`. That is the order in which they decide, strongest first, as `docs/design/controls.md` §3 sets out. A field given as `none`, or not listed, is absent. Facts after `review`, such as a Vault login or a user's earlier request, set up the rest of the scene. `review: on, returns allow` (or `ask`, or `deny`) means the automatic review is on and gives that verdict when it is reached, which is only when no field before it decides; with `review: off`, an action that nothing decides asks first. A grant that has expired or been revoked is absent, so the next matching call is decided by the order again.

Where a row says "Clawtrate's choice", the number or rule is Clawtrate's own acceptance boundary.

A row whose behavior cell ends "(split from F-0xx)" was split out of that row; the original id keeps the first part.

| F-201 | Proactive | both | Each Bot's check-ins can be turned off or put on a schedule. | better: a per-Bot "Check-ins" setting with Off, Daily, As needed and Every… (FC-11); "As needed" checks every 30 minutes; the setting says the prompt asks the Bot to stay silent unless something needs the person only when the prompt in effect is known to be the gateway's built-in one, and for a custom, inherited custom or unreadable prompt says the cadence alone; saving one Bot's setting keeps the check-ins of every Bot that has them, by writing their current cadence in the same change. Confirmed on OpenClaw 2026.10.6: with no per-Bot setting only `agents.defaults.heartbeat.agentId` has a job; writing one Bot's setting removed the default Bot's job and removing it brought it back; `every: "24h"` gave `everyMs` 86,400,000; `every: "0m"` keeps the job with `enabled: false` (that is Off); `cron.update` on the job is refused ("system-owned monitor jobs cannot be edited by cron clients"). Daily is an `every` value; "As needed" is not a gateway mode, so it is `every: "30m"`; the gateway's own prompt asks for `NO_REPLY` when nothing needs attention (asked, not guaranteed), and a custom prompt is the Bot's to keep. OpenClaw 2026.11.10 retired heartbeats: its doctor turns each into an ordinary `agentTurn` job and removes every `heartbeat` setting, `cron.list` marks no job as a check-in, `config.schema.lookup {path: "agents.defaults.heartbeat"}` answers "config schema path not found" (read tier), and a `config.patch` with a `heartbeat` key is refused whole ("Unrecognized key"). docs/decisions.md, "Check-ins". | desktop done |

| F-223 | Connectors | iOS | Device apps (Calendar, Contacts, Location, Alarms, Reminders, Home) list under "This iPhone" with "Allow", "Allowed" or "Denied". | same | partial: Clawtrate can serve as a node: connect with `role:"node"`, `platform`, `deviceFamily`, `commands` and `permissions`, answer `node.invoke.request {id, command, paramsJSON, timeoutMs}` with `node.invoke.result {id, nodeId, ok, payload}` (live), and be paired with `node.pair.approve` (`operator.pairing` with no commands; plus `operator.write` for ordinary declared commands; plus `operator.admin` for `system.run.prepare`, `system.run`, `system.which`, `system.execApprovals.get`, `system.execApprovals.set`, `browser.proxy`, `browser.proxy.upload.v1`, `fs.listDir` or `terminal.upload`; silent pairing was shown on loopback). Allowed commands are the platform defaults (iOS: `location.get`, `contacts.search`, `calendar.events`, `reminders.list`, `photos.latest`, `device.info`, `device.status`, `system.notify` and others; macOS adds `device.apps`); dangerous commands and any others need `gateway.nodes.commands.allow` (admin config; a custom command was refused live) or a plugin node-invoke policy (`api.registerNodeInvokePolicy`). Apps outside the defaults, such as Home or alarms, need the allowlist. Allow, Denied and "Open Settings" are the node's own permission UI: the gateway does not use the reported `permissions` to admit `node.invoke`, so the node refuses what it cannot serve. When pairing requires a decision (`docs/research/device-node.md` §1, live, with the shared token): a node's first connect is a device request for the node role, which only `operator.admin` or `openclaw devices approve` on the gateway's computer approves; its command surface is a second request, which the node's own device, connected with its device token, approves at `operator.pairing` plus `operator.write`, and another device only with admin. Clawtrate's node needs a credential of its own (§1.7, live): a device holding only its operator token cannot connect as a node. On a token-authenticated loopback development gateway, admin requested a node-only code with `publicUrl` set to the current gateway URL. Read and write plus pairing were refused for scope. A fresh node key presenting the code paired and its approved commands were read back. Declared commands outside the lists are dropped silently; `node.describe` reads back what was kept, as a snapshot of that moment. | todo |

| F-347 | Approvals | iOS | If permission was denied before, the card offers "Open Settings". (split from F-185) | same | partial: Clawtrate can serve as a node: connect with `role:"node"`, `platform`, `deviceFamily`, `commands` and `permissions`, answer `node.invoke.request {id, command, paramsJSON, timeoutMs}` with `node.invoke.result {id, nodeId, ok, payload}` (live), and be paired with `node.pair.approve` (`operator.pairing` with no commands; plus `operator.write` for ordinary declared commands; plus `operator.admin` for `system.run.prepare`, `system.run`, `system.which`, `system.execApprovals.get`, `system.execApprovals.set`, `browser.proxy`, `browser.proxy.upload.v1`, `fs.listDir` or `terminal.upload`; silent pairing was shown on loopback). Allowed commands are the platform defaults (iOS: `location.get`, `contacts.search`, `calendar.events`, `reminders.list`, `photos.latest`, `device.info`, `device.status`, `system.notify` and others; macOS adds `device.apps`); dangerous commands and any others need `gateway.nodes.commands.allow` (admin config; a custom command was refused live) or a plugin node-invoke policy (`api.registerNodeInvokePolicy`). Allow, Denied and "Open Settings" are the node's own permission UI: the gateway does not use the reported `permissions` to admit `node.invoke`, so the node refuses what it cannot serve. When pairing requires a decision (`docs/research/device-node.md` §1, live, with the shared token): a node's first connect is a device request for the node role, which only `operator.admin` or `openclaw devices approve` on the gateway's computer approves; its command surface is a second request, which the node's own device, connected with its device token, approves at `operator.pairing` plus `operator.write`, and another device only with admin. Clawtrate's node needs a credential of its own (§1.7, live): a device holding only its operator token cannot connect as a node. On a token-authenticated loopback development gateway, admin requested a node-only code with `publicUrl` set to the current gateway URL. Read and write plus pairing were refused for scope. A fresh node key presenting the code paired and its approved commands were read back. Declared commands outside the lists are dropped silently; `node.describe` reads back what was kept, as a snapshot of that moment. | todo |
| F-348 | Approvals | iOS | A request for a phone permission waits up to five minutes for Allow. (split from F-185) | better: after 5 minutes with no answer the card reads "Timed out", and the Bot continues without the phone app and says so. | partial: Clawtrate can keep its own permission card and show "Timed out" after five minutes. OpenClaw 2026.10.6 ends `node.invoke` at its call limit, 30 seconds by default, and ignores late answers (live, research/device-node.md §3). An unanswered call can return `TIMEOUT: node invoke timed out`. The node may send `NO_ANSWER_ON_DEVICE` while the call is still pending. The gateway does not hold the call until the card expires, resume it or guarantee that the bot continues and says so (source, §4.3). | todo |

| F-451 | Approvals | iOS | If allowed within an hour, the Bot can pick the request back up. (split from F-348) | better: fixture — gateway denial: none; deny: none; ask: none; allow: none; review: on, returns allow → allowing within 1 hour of the request re-evaluates the order and runs the original device operation once, and the card reads "Resumed". Variant: a gateway denial for the operation is added during the wait → allowing within the hour does not run it, and the card reads "Blocked by policy". | none: OpenClaw 2026.10.6 has no supported way for a node to resume the original bot operation once after the call ends. A late result is ignored (live, research/device-node.md §3). The gateway's pending foreground queue excludes a bot's calls, and the node cannot start a bot turn (source, §§4.3, 5). Removing an allowlist command can end a currently waiting call with `POLICY_CHANGED` and deny a later call (live, §6). Neither result re-evaluates or resumes the ended call. A local grant can apply to a future request only (F-452). | todo |
| F-452 | Approvals | iOS | Allowing more than an hour after the request grants later requests only. (split from F-348) | better: allowing more than 1 hour after the request grants the permission for later requests only, and the original operation does not run. | partial: Clawtrate can serve as a node: connect with `role:"node"`, `platform`, `deviceFamily`, `commands` and `permissions`, answer `node.invoke.request {id, command, paramsJSON, timeoutMs}` with `node.invoke.result {id, nodeId, ok, payload}` (live), and be paired with `node.pair.approve` (`operator.pairing` with no commands; plus `operator.write` for ordinary declared commands; plus `operator.admin` for `system.run.prepare`, `system.run`, `system.which`, `system.execApprovals.get`, `system.execApprovals.set`, `browser.proxy`, `browser.proxy.upload.v1`, `fs.listDir` or `terminal.upload`; silent pairing was shown on loopback). Allowed commands are the platform defaults (iOS: `location.get`, `contacts.search`, `calendar.events`, `reminders.list`, `photos.latest`, `device.info`, `device.status`, `system.notify` and others; macOS adds `device.apps`); dangerous commands and any others need `gateway.nodes.commands.allow` (admin config; a custom command was refused live) or a plugin node-invoke policy (`api.registerNodeInvokePolicy`). An iOS node that answers `NODE_BACKGROUND_UNAVAILABLE` for `camera.*`, `screen.*`, `talk.*` or foreground-restricted plugin commands has the operator's request queued: `QUEUED_UNTIL_FOREGROUND` with a `queuedActionId`, fetched by the node with `node.pending.pull` and `ack`, kept 10 minutes, 64 per node (source); agent-runtime calls and forwarded approval authority are excluded, so granting the permission for later requests only, without running the original operation, remains unbuilt. When pairing requires a decision (`docs/research/device-node.md` §1, live, with the shared token): a node's first connect is a device request for the node role, which only `operator.admin` or `openclaw devices approve` on the gateway's computer approves; its command surface is a second request, which the node's own device, connected with its device token, approves at `operator.pairing` plus `operator.write`, and another device only with admin. Clawtrate's node needs a credential of its own (§1.7, live): a device holding only its operator token cannot connect as a node. On a token-authenticated loopback development gateway, admin requested a node-only code with `publicUrl` set to the current gateway URL. Read and write plus pairing were refused for scope. A fresh node key presenting the code paired and its approved commands were read back. Declared commands outside the lists are dropped silently; `node.describe` reads back what was kept, as a snapshot of that moment. | todo |

| F-468 | Working UI | both | A run's state, and the messages sent during it, follow the rules for questions, approvals, queueing and Stop. (split from F-089) | better: parameterized fixture over the run's rules in `docs/design/conversation.md` sections 13 to 16 and `docs/decisions.md`, "Queue and hold": a successful run goes from running to completed and its status line is removed; a steer sent while only approval cards are pending joins the run and leaves them pending (F-169); while a question card is pending, a typed message is held and not sent, "Send now" is not offered, the question stays open and the run stays waiting; when the last pending question is answered, dismissed or expires, held messages are released in order; after a confirmed Stop nothing starts by itself. | partial: close the run's pending exec approvals with `exec.approval.resolve` and plugin approvals with `plugin.approval.resolve`, or use `approval.resolve` with the matching kind, all with `deny` (`operator.approvals`); steer with `chat.send` `queueMode` `steer` (`operator.write`), with F-096's caveat. A steer sent while the run waits on an approval is held by the gateway (`pendingInputs` `queued`), leaves the approval pending, and joins once it is allowed; on a Stop it is withdrawn first with `chat.abort` naming its own id, so it never runs ([the steering research](research/steer-stop.md)). Question state comes from `question.requested` and `question.resolved` (`operator.questions`), but an admitted ordinary text steer into the same session can be consumed as the pending question's answer before steering. This requires a current pending question, valid answer authority and an accepted answer; rejected answers can leave it open, and image input can cancel it instead. The public client contract has no mapped way to send this ordinary steer while guaranteeing the question remains pending. So the client holds the message while a question is pending, as the target says; holding needs nothing from the gateway. | desktop done |

The switch reads the result back before it says the skill is in place, and says so when the gateway still withholds the tools. So "off by default" means a deny on every other bot, added to every new bot, and checked for bots created elsewhere; or a plugin tool factory that returns tools only for enabled bots (C.5). A bot created elsewhere has the tools until Clawtrate notices and denies them; preventing that needs a gateway-side default or the plugin factory. Skill: `agents.entries.<id>.skills` allowlist (`operator.admin`); `skills.install` installs a declared install operation, not an uploaded skill, so Clawtrate's skill needs a distribution path. The check via the scripted provider's request record works (A.11 logs offered tool-server tool names). | todo |

An unknown model is not refused at launch. Not observed against real T3 Code; F-482 and F-483 must be in place first. | todo |

Resolving its approval card is `plugin.approval.*` (`operator.approvals`). T3 Code itself limits only by access level and mode (`t3-mode-limit` header), not by project or provider (B.1). "Not sent" is detected from the tool `result` with `details.status:"blocked"` (with `deniedReason`, default `"plugin-before-tool-call"`, and `reason`), not from `isError` (B.5; source, not observed). | todo |

Turning it on reveals the entry of F-482.
